## 1. Purpose and scope

The plant values are computed, not typed in. This page describes how: the independent inputs (drivers), the balance solver that turns them into stream rates and duties, the sizing library, and the gate that compares the solver with the graph. It also lists every driver with its source, the published references, and the values that still need verification by test. It does not size any unit itself.

## 2. Terms

- Driver: an independent input in `flow_graph/param/drivers.json`, with units and a source.
- Misfit: the deviation of a value on the graph from the value the solver computes.
- Closure: the mass or energy balance of a solved unit, which must sum to zero within the solver tolerance.
- BMED: bipolar-membrane electrodialysis. EW: electrowinning. MVR: mechanical vapour recompression.

## 3. Framework

1. Drivers (`flow_graph/param/drivers.json`, loader `drivers.py`) are the independent inputs. Each has units and a published reference key, a register entry or an owner basis. A value that no published source fixes carries `needs_verification` and `verification_test`, and the loader fails if either is missing.
2. The balance solver (`solver.py`) computes edge rates and duties from the drivers with a transfer function for each unit: stoichiometry, splits, Faraday's law and energy balances. Its `check()` fails on a closure error of a solved unit and lists the misfits against the graph.
3. The sizing library (`sizing/`) holds the equations: electrochemistry (Faraday current, cathode count, direct-current power), heat (sensible duty, mean temperature difference, area), lines (bore from velocity, nominal size), power (array size, three-phase current, breaker, rectifier channel, trim heaters), tanks (hydrostatic load, volume, agitator power, plate and beam stress, pump power), relief (API 520), vents (API 2000), scrubbers (packed column), evaporators and the HCl vapour-liquid equilibrium. Each function names its equation and reference.
4. The scripts in `docs-authority/` call the library with the drivers, and the regenerator rewrites their outputs on each commit. `write_graph.py` writes solved values into the graph: stage `process` for the acid loop, BMED, electrowinning, the rectifier and the bus balance, and stage `power` for the array, storage and inverters of AQS-ELEC-007.
5. The gate `flow_graph/param/test_param_misfit.py` fails when a solved unit does not close or a compared graph value deviates from the solver by more than 1 %.

| solver: leach acid (case A), BMED, EVAP_ACID and EVAP_BASE, electrowinning currents, rectifier | electrochem, constants | feed rate, class A oxides, leach extent, HCl stoichiometry, BMED specific energy and molarity, loop concentrations, MVR energy |

## 5. Solver closure

All solved units close.

## 7. Acid-loop decision (X-PAR-04)

1. BMED runs at 2 M product, inside the published limit of about 3.6 M acid for electrodialysis with bipolar membranes (EDBM_WHEY_2021). The stack power is 5.63 kW from 1.40 kWh per kilogram of NaOH at a triplet voltage of 1.67 V (AQS-DUTY-068); the 2 M whey figure of 6.7 kWh/kg would give 26.93 kW.
2. EVAP_ACID concentrates HCl from 7.07 wt% to 20 wt%, below the HCl-water azeotrope at 20.2 wt% (PERRY9_HCL). The feed is 1304.8 kg/d, the water removed 865.2 kg/d and the power 1.50 kW at 72.0 kWh/t (VOGELBUSCH_MVR upper bound).
3. EVAP_BASE concentrates NaOH from 7.41 wt% to 20 wt%. The feed is 1301.7 kg/d, the water removed 801.9 kg/d and the power 1.33 kW.
4. The condensate returns to the BMED stack as make-up water (IA-EVAP_ACID-COND, IA-EVAP_BASE-COND). Both evaporators have 480 V feeds (SF-EVAP_ACID, SF-EVAP_BASE) and heat edges to HEAT_SINK.
5. Electrowinning runs at 2.12 V per cell and a current efficiency of 0.85 (USBM_CL_EW chloride basis), with a bus drop of 0.3 V and a rectifier efficiency of 0.8. This gives 12781 A, 30.93 kW direct current and 38.66 kW alternating current.
6. The power change is carried on IA-PV-01, and the array, storage and inverters follow the power sizing AQS-ELEC-007.
7. NaOH balance (owner decision 2026-10-15, X-PAR-06): precipitation takes 2412 mol/d, one NaOH per chloride (GREENWOOD); the glass digest takes 109.8 mol/d, two NaOH per SiO2 dissolved as a bound; BMED makes 2412 mol/d. The make-up is 4.39 kg/d of NaOH flake on IA-NAOH-MU from NORTH_DOCK, the worst case with no sodium credit, with an equal-mass purge on IA-NA-PURGE. EVAP_BASE is sized from the BMED output plus the make-up.
